# Bill Gates — Books Guru recommendations — Books Guru source review

2026-10-21, Europe/Warsaw. Source 148: [submitted page](https://books-guru.com/experts/bill-gates).

The user requested that previously covered titles and duplicates be documented without deeper reassessment. Only unique new titles receive the full current gate and score assessment. Existing scores and status are retained; this source is appended as provenance, not independent evidence of quality.

Capture: 246 cards; advertised 246. All book article cards in each captured server-rendered HTML page; followed the observed rel=next pagination link until absent. No additional card count inferred from visible browser limits. Source descriptions and rating figures are third-party claims; mismatched descriptions were observed. New-book evidence is checked separately.

### 13 Things that Don't Make Sense — Michael Brooks

**recommended; moderate eligibility confidence.** Published nonfiction survey of unresolved scientific problems with sustained exposition or narrative; not an exam text, specialist reference or procedural manual. Anomalies provide questions and scientific context, but uneven resolution and specialist novelty constrain value; no serious gate is established.

Sources: 148.

| Gate | Finding |
|---|---|
| BOOK_FORM | Published book confirmed in the cited primary publication record. |
| FICTION | Nonfiction survey of unresolved scientific problems. |
| REFERENCE | Sustained survey of unresolved scientific problems intended for readers beyond procedural training or specialist lookup. |
| V1 | No serious central evidential failure established in the inspected material; Research reporting covers diverse anomalies but selection emphasizes puzzles over resolution. |
| V2 | No serious substitution of allegiance for evidence established; the identified limitations remain scored. |
| V3 | Uncertainty and anomaly detection offer modest reusable research heuristics; Introduces candidate explanations without deeply deriving competing mechanisms. |
| V4 | No primary dependence on passing publicity established; Changing anomaly status reduces durability of several chapters. |
| V5 | No central authoritative doctrine or specifically evidenced extremist advocacy established; subject and reform proposals alone are insufficient. |

[Evidence 1](https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/18963/13-things-that-dont-make-sense-by-michael-brooks/).

<a id="bga78a5b9c3f"></a>
### Hyperbole and a Half — Allie Brosh

**recommended; moderate eligibility confidence.** Published nonfiction illustrated autobiographical humor with sustained exposition or narrative; not an exam text, specialist reference or procedural manual. The included Depression Part Two essay distinguishes loss of emotional access from sadness and explains a recurring communication failure: positive reassurance assumes feelings the sufferer cannot access, so further reassurance increases misunderstanding and isolation. This model survives removal of personal details and defeats V3 despite low overall analytical density.

| V3 | Pass: Depression Part Two supplies a specific generalizable model of anhedonia and failed reassurance, beyond personal names or incidental humor. The publisher confirms this essay is in the nominated collection. |

| V5 | No central authoritative doctrine or specifically evidenced extremist advocacy established; subject and reform proposals alone are insufficient. |
